{"file_path": "C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/lsatu-reading-center/src/features/reading-center/components/BookmarksDialog.tsx", "content": "import React from \"react\";\nimport { List, Trash2 } from \"lucide-react\";\nimport { Dialog, DialogContent, DialogDescription, DialogHeader, DialogTitle } from \"@/components/ui/dialog\";\nimport { Button } from \"@/components/ui/button\";\nimport type { Bookmark, Token } from \"../lib/types\";\n\nexport function BookmarksDialog({\n  open,\n  onOpenChange,\n  bookmarks,\n  tokens,\n  onJump,\n  onDelete,\n}: {\n  open: boolean;\n  onOpenChange: (open: boolean) => void;\n  bookmarks: Bookmark[];\n  tokens: Token[];\n  onJump: (wordIndex: number) => void;\n  onDelete: (id: string) => void;\n}): React.ReactElement {\n  const sorted = [...bookmarks].sort((a, b) => a.word_index - b.word_index);\n\n  return (\n    <Dialog open={open} onOpenChange={onOpenChange}>\n      <DialogContent className=\"max-w-lg\">\n        <DialogHeader>\n          <DialogTitle>Bookmarks</DialogTitle>\n          <DialogDescription className=\"sr-only\">Saved places in this passage.</DialogDescription>\n        </DialogHeader>\n        {sorted.length === 0 ? (\n          <div className=\"flex flex-col items-center gap-2 py-10 text-center\">\n            <List className=\"h-8 w-8 text-muted-foreground\" />\n            <div className=\"font-semibold\">No bookmarks yet</div>\n            <p className=\"text-sm text-muted-foreground\">To add one, pause and click any word.</p>\n          </div>\n        ) : (\n          <ul className=\"max-h-[60vh] divide-y overflow-y-auto\">\n            {sorted.map((b) => {\n              const before = tokens.slice(Math.max(0, b.word_index - 6), b.word_index).map((t) => t.text).join(\" \");\n              const after = tokens.slice(b.word_index + 1, b.word_index + 7).map((t) => t.text).join(\" \");\n              return (\n                <li key={b.id} className=\"flex items-start gap-2 py-2\">\n                  <button\n                    type=\"button\"\n                    className=\"min-w-0 flex-1 rounded-md px-2 py-1 text-left hover:bg-muted\"\n                    onClick={() => {\n                      onJump(b.word_index);\n                      onOpenChange(false);\n                    }}\n                  >\n                    <div className=\"text-sm\">\n                      {before} <strong>{tokens[b.word_index]?.text}</strong> {after}\n                    </div>\n                    <div className=\"mt-0.5 text-xs text-muted-foreground\">\n                      {new Date(b.created_at).toLocaleDateString()}\n                    </div>\n                  </button>\n                  <Button variant=\"ghost\" size=\"icon\" aria-label=\"Delete bookmark\" onClick={() => onDelete(b.id)}>\n                    <Trash2 className=\"h-4 w-4\" />\n                  </Button>\n                </li>\n              );\n            })}\n          </ul>\n        )}\n      </DialogContent>\n    </Dialog>\n  );\n}\n"}

{"file_path": "C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/lsatu-reading-center/src/features/reading-center/components/CompletionCard.tsx", "content": "import React from \"react\";\nimport { CheckCircle2 } from \"lucide-react\";\nimport { Button } from \"@/components/ui/button\";\nimport { effectiveWpm } from \"../lib/timing\";\n\nfunction formatClock(ms: number): string {\n  const total = Math.round(ms / 1000);\n  return `${Math.floor(total / 60)}:${String(total % 60).padStart(2, \"0\")}`;\n}\n\nexport function CompletionCard({\n  wordsRead,\n  activeMs,\n  onReadAgain,\n  onBack,\n}: {\n  wordsRead: number;\n  activeMs: number;\n  onReadAgain: () => void;\n  onBack: () => void;\n}): React.ReactElement {\n  const stats: [string, string][] = [\n    [\"Words read\", String(wordsRead)],\n    [\"Time\", formatClock(activeMs)],\n    [\"Effective WPM\", String(effectiveWpm(wordsRead, activeMs))],\n  ];\n  return (\n    <div className=\"absolute inset-0 z-20 flex items-center justify-center bg-black/40 p-4\">\n      <div className=\"w-full max-w-sm rounded-lg border bg-background p-6 text-foreground shadow-lg\">\n        <div className=\"flex items-center gap-2 text-lg font-semibold\">\n          <CheckCircle2 className=\"h-5 w-5 text-primary\" />\n          Passage complete\n        </div>\n        <dl className=\"mt-4 grid grid-cols-3 gap-3 text-center\">\n          {stats.map(([label, value]) => (\n            <div key={label}>\n              <dt className=\"text-xs text-muted-foreground\">{label}</dt>\n              <dd className=\"text-xl font-semibold tabular-nums\">{value}</dd>\n            </div>\n          ))}\n        </dl>\n        <div className=\"mt-6 flex justify-end gap-2\">\n          <Button variant=\"outline\" onClick={onBack}>Back to library</Button>\n          <Button onClick={onReadAgain}>Read again</Button>\n        </div>\n      </div>\n    </div>\n  );\n}\n"}

{"file_path": "C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/lsatu-reading-center/src/features/reading-center/components/GoalDialog.tsx", "content": "import React, { useEffect, useState } from \"react\";\nimport { Dialog, DialogContent, DialogDescription, DialogFooter, DialogHeader, DialogTitle } from \"@/components/ui/dialog\";\nimport { Slider } from \"@/components/ui/slider\";\nimport { Button } from \"@/components/ui/button\";\n\nconst round50 = (v: number): number => Math.round(v / 50) * 50;\n\n/** Recommended goal: three times the current speed, at least 300; 600 with no data yet. */\nexport function recommendedGoal(currentSpeed: number): number {\n  return currentSpeed > 0 ? Math.min(5000, Math.max(300, round50(3 * currentSpeed))) : 600;\n}\n\nexport function GoalDialog({\n  open,\n  onOpenChange,\n  value,\n  currentSpeed,\n  onSet,\n}: {\n  open: boolean;\n  onOpenChange: (open: boolean) => void;\n  value: number;\n  currentSpeed: number;\n  onSet: (goal: number) => void;\n}): React.ReactElement {\n  const [local, setLocal] = useState(value);\n  useEffect(() => {\n    if (open) setLocal(value);\n  }, [open, value]);\n\n  return (\n    <Dialog open={open} onOpenChange={onOpenChange}>\n      <DialogContent className=\"max-w-md\">\n        <DialogHeader>\n          <DialogTitle>Set your goal</DialogTitle>\n          <DialogDescription>\n            The average reader manages about 200 words per minute. A good first goal is 600.\n          </DialogDescription>\n        </DialogHeader>\n        <div className=\"space-y-3 py-2\">\n          <div className=\"text-center text-4xl font-semibold tabular-nums\">{local} WPM</div>\n          <Slider value={[local]} min={50} max={5000} step={50} onValueChange={([v]) => setLocal(v)} aria-label=\"Goal WPM\" />\n        </div>\n        <DialogFooter className=\"gap-2\">\n          <Button variant=\"ghost\" onClick={() => onOpenChange(false)}>Cancel</Button>\n          <Button variant=\"outline\" onClick={() => setLocal(recommendedGoal(currentSpeed))}>Recommend</Button>\n          <Button\n            onClick={() => {\n              onSet(local);\n              onOpenChange(false);\n            }}\n          >\n            Set\n          </Button>\n        </DialogFooter>\n      </DialogContent>\n    </Dialog>\n  );\n}\n"}

Math.round(list.reduce((a, s) => a + s.active_ms, 0) / 60000) };\n    });\n  }, [sessions, metric]);\n\n  const yLabel = metric === \"speed\" ? \"WPM\" : metric === \"focus\" ? \"%\" : \"min\";\n\n  return (\n    <div className=\"mx-auto w-full max-w-5xl px-5 py-6 sm:px-6\">\n      <Link to={RC_LIBRARY_PATH} className=\"inline-flex items-center gap-1.5 text-sm text-black/55 hover:text-black\">\n        <ArrowLeft className=\"h-4 w-4\" /> Reading Center\n      </Link>\n      <div className=\"mt-3 flex flex-wrap items-center gap-3\">\n        <h1 className=\"font-display text-2xl tracking-tight\">Your reading</h1>\n        <div className=\"ml-auto flex items-center gap-2\">\n          <Select value={period} onValueChange={setPeriod}>\n            <SelectTrigger className=\"h-9 w-36 border-black/15 bg-white/40\"><SelectValue /></SelectTrigger>\n            <SelectContent>\n              {STAT_PERIODS.map((p) => (\n                <SelectItem key={p.value} value={p.value}>{p.label}</SelectItem>\n              ))}\n            </SelectContent>\n          </Select>\n          <button\n            type=\"button\"\n            onClick={() => setGoalOpen(true)}\n            className=\"h-9 border border-black bg-black px-3 text-sm font-medium text-white hover:bg-black/85\"\n          >\n            Set goal\n          </button>\n        </div>\n      </div>\n\n      <section className=\"mt-6 border border-black/12 bg-white/25 p-5\">\n        <h2 className=\"text-[11px] font-medium uppercase tracking-[0.2em] text-black/45\">Vital value={prefs.goal_wpm} />\n        </div>\n      </section>\n\n      <section className=\"mt-5 border border-black/12 bg-white/25 p-5\">\n        <div className=\"flex items-center justify-between        <SelectTrigger className=\"h-9 w-44 border-black/15 bg-white/40\"><SelectValue /></SelectTrigger>\n            <SelectContent>\n              <SelectItem value=\"speed\">Reading </Select>\n        </div>\n\n        {sessionsQuery.isLoading ? \"\");\n    setAuthor(passage?.author ?? \"\");\n    setCoverUrl(passage?.cover_url ?? \"\");\n    setBody(passage?.body ?? \"\");\n    upsert.reset();\n    // eslint-disable-next-line react-hooks/exhaustive-deps\n  }, [open, passage]);\n\n  const words = countWords(body);\n  const canSave = title.trim() !== \"\" && body.trim() !== \"\" && !upsert.isPending;\n\n  const save = () => {\n    const fields = {\n      title: title.trim(),\n      author: author.trim() || null,\n      cover_url: coverUrl.trim() || null,\n      body: body.trim(),\n    };\n    // The slug is fixed at creation so reader links and saved progress never break.\n    const payload = passage\n      ? { id: passage.id, slug: passage.slug, ...fields }\n      : { slug: slugify(fields.title), sort_order: nextSortOrder, is_published: true, ...fields };\n    upsert.mutate(payload, { onSuccess: () => onOpenChange(false) });\n  };\n\n  return (\n    <Dialog open={open} onOpenChange={onOpenChange}>\n      <DialogContent className=\"max-h-[95vh] max-w-3xl overflow-y-auto\">\n        <DialogHeader>\n          <DialogTitle>{passage ? \"Edit passage\" : \"New passage\"}</DialogTitle>\n          <DialogDescription className=\"sr-only\">Title, author, cover and text of the passage.</DialogDescription>\n        </DialogHeader>\n        <div className=\"space-y-4\">\n          <div className=\"space-y-1.5\">\n            <Label htmlFor=\"rc-title\">Title</Label>\n            <Input id=\"rc-title\" value={title} onChange={(e) => setTitle(e.target.value)} required />\n          </div>\n          <div className=\"grid gap-4 sm:grid-cols-2\">\n            <div className=\"space-y-1.5\">\n              <Label htmlFor=\"rc-author\">Author (optional)</Label>\n              <Input id=\"rc-author\" value={author} onChange={(e) => setAuthor(e.target.value)} />\n            </div>\n            <div className=\"space-y-1.5\">\n              <Label htmlFor=\"rc-cover\">Cover URL (optional)</Label>\n              <Input id=\"rc-cover\" value={coverUrl} onChange={(e) => setCoverUrl(e.target.value)} />\n            </div>\n          </div>\n          <div className=\"space-y-1.5\">\n            <Label htmlFor=\"rc-body\">Body</Label>\n            <Textarea id=\"rc-body\" value={body} onChange={(e) => setBody(e.target.value)} className=\"min-h-[50vh]\" />\n            <p className=\"text-xs text-muted-foreground\">Leave a blank line between paragraphs.</p>\n            <p className=\"text-xs text-muted-foreground\">\n              {words} words · about {estimateMinutes(words, 200)} min at 200 WPM\n            </p>\n          </div>\n          {upsert.error && <p className=\"text-sm text-red-600\">{upsert.error.message}</p>}\n        </div>\n        <DialogFooter>\n          <Button variant=\"ghost\" onClick={() => onOpenChange(false)}>Cancel</Button>\n          <Button onClick={save} disabled={!canSave}>{upsert.isPending ? \"Saving\" : \"Save\"}</Button>\n        </DialogFooter>\n      </DialogContent>\n    </Dialog>\n  );\n}\n"}

(\n        <p className=\"mt-8 text-sm text-black/55\">No passages yet.</p>\n      ) : (\n        <table className=\"mt-6 w-full border border-black/12 bg-white/25 text-sm\">\n          <thead className=\"text-left text-[11px] uppercase tracking-[0.14em] text-black/45\">\n            <tr className=\"border-b border-black/10\">\n              <th className=\"w-20 px-3 py-2 font-medium\">Order</th>\n              <th className=\"px-3 py-2 font-medium\">Title</th>\n              <th className=\"w-24 px-3 py-2 font-medium\">Words</th>\n              <th className=\"w-24 px-3 py-2 font-medium\">Published</th>\n              <th className=\"w-24 px-3 py-2\" />\n            </tr>\n          </thead>\n          <tbody>\n            {passages.map((p, i) => (\n              <tr key={p.id} className=\"border-b border-black/8 last:border-0\">\n                <td className=\"px-3 py-1.5\">\n                  <div className=\"flex\">\n                    <Button variant=\"ghost\" size=\"icon\" className=\"h-7 w-7\" aria-label={`Move ${p.title} up`} disabled={i === 0 || reorder.isPending} onClick={() => move(i, i - 1)}>\n                      <ArrowUp className=\"h-3.5 w-3.5\" />\n                    </Button>\n                    <Button variant=\"ghost\" size=\"icon\" className=\"h-7 w-7\" aria-label={`Move ${p.title} down`} disabled={i === passages.length - 1 || reorder.isPending} onClick={() => move(i, i + 1)}>\n                      <ArrowDown className=\"h-3.5 w-3.5\" />\n                    </Button>\n                  </div>\n                </td>\n                <td className=\"px-3 py-1.5 font-medium\">{p.title}</td>\n                <td className=\"px-3 py-1.5 tabular-nums text-black/60\">{p.word_count}</td>\n                <td className=\"px-3 py-1.5\">\n                  <Switch\n                    checked={p.is_published}\n                    aria-label={`Published: ${p.title}`}\n                    onCheckedChange={(v) =>\n                      upsert.mutate(\n                        { id: p.id, slug: p.slug, title: p.title, body: p.body, is_published: v },\n                        { onError: (e) => toast.error(e.message) },\n                      )\n                    }\n                  />\n                </td>\n                <td className=\"px-3 py-1.5\">\n                  <div className=\"flex justify-end\">\n                    <Button variant=\"ghost\" size=\"icon\" className=\"h-8 w-8\" aria-label={`Edit ${p.title}`} onClick={() => setEditing(p)}>\n                      <Pencil className=\"h-4 w-4\" />\n                    </Button>\n                    <Button variant=\"ghost\" size=\"icon\" className=\"h-8 w-8\" aria-label={`Delete ${p.title}`} onClick={() => setDeleting(p)}>\n                      <Trash2 className=\"h-4 w-4\" />\n                    </Button>\n                  </div>\n                </td>\n              </tr>\n            ))}\n          </tbody>\n        </table>\n      )}\n\n      <PassageEditor\n        open={editing !== undefined}\n        onOpenChange={(o) => !o && setEditing(undefined)}\n        passage={editing ?? null}\n        nextSortOrder={nextSortOrder}\n      />\n      <DeletePassageDialog passage={deleting} onClose={() => setDeleting(null)} />\n    </div>\n  );\n}\n"}

116:  $rc$The rationalizing of society can be conceptualized as the pursuit of efficiency, predictability, calculability, and control through technology. But rational systems inevitably spawn a series of irrationalities that result in the compromising and perhaps even the undermining of their rationality. Fast-food restaurants, which epitomize the rational model, proffer the fastest means of getting from a hungry state to a sated one, without surprises, at low cost, in a carnival-like setting suggesting that fun awaits the consumer at each visit. The wholesomeness of the food seems an insignificant consideration. Whereas in the past, working people were prepared to spend up to an hour preparing dinner, they now are impatient if a meal is not on the table within ten minutes. (For their part, some fast-food restaurants have developed chairs that become uncomfortable after about twenty minutes, to ensure that diners do not stay long.) Fast-food restaurants have preferentially recruited adolescent help, at least until recently, because this age group adjusts more easily than adults do to surrendering their autonomy to machines, rules, and procedures. Few skills are required on the job, so workers are asked to use only a minute portion of their abilities. This policy is irrational from the standpoint of the organization, since it could obtain much more from its employees for the money (however negligible) it pays them. These minimal skill demands are also irrational from the perspective of the employees, who are not allowed to think or to respond creatively to the demands of the work. These restrictions lead to high levels of resentment, job dissatisfaction, alienation, absenteeism, and turnover among workers in fast-food franchises. In fact, these businesses have the highest turnover rate of any industry in the U.S. The entire workforce of the fast-food industry turns over three times in a year. Although the simple, repetitive nature of the work makes it easy to replace those who leave, the organization would clearly benefit from keeping employees longer. The costs of hiring and training are magnified when the turnover rate is extraordinarily high.

Paragraphs are\n * separated by a blank line. Passages are matched by slug and never deleted.\n *\n * Credentials come from ~/ai-os/.env (git-ignored), signing in as the admin\n * user, the same way scripts/assign.ts does. No key is ever printed.\n */\nimport fs from \"node:fs\";\nimport path from \"node:path\";\nimport { fileURLToPath } from \"node:url\";\nimport { supabase } from \"@/integrations/supabase/client\";\nimport { countWords, slugify } from \"../src/features/reading-center/lib/tokenize\";\n\nconst REPO = path.resolve(path.dirname(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)), \"..\");\n\nfunction readEnv(file: string): Record<string, string> {\n  const out: Record<string, string> = {};\n  if (!fs.existsSync(file)) return out;\n  for (const line of fs.readFileSync(file, \"utf8\").split(/\\r?\\n/)) {\n    const t = line.trim();\n    if (!t || t.startsWith(\"#\")) continue;\n    const i = t.indexOf(\"=\");\n    if (i < 0) continue;\n    out[t.slice(0, i).trim()] = t.slice(i + 1).trim().replace(/^[\"']|[\"']$/g, \"\");\n  }\n  return out;\n}\n\nconst env = {\n  ...readEnv(path.resolve(REPO, \"../../.env\")),\n  ...readEnv(path.join(REPO, \".env\")),\n};\n\n// The rc tables are not in the generated types yet (same cast as lib/db.ts).\n// eslint-disable-next-line @typescript-eslint/no-explicit-any\nconst rc = (table: string) => (supabase as any).from(table);\n\ninterface ParsedFile {\n  file: string;\n  slug: string;\n  title: string;\n  author: string | null;\n  cover_url: string | null;\n  sort_order: number | null;\n  body: string;\n  word_count: number;\n}\n\ninterface ExistingRow {\n  id: string;\n  slug: string;\n  title: string;\n  author: string | null;\n  cover_url: string | null;\n  sort_order: number;\n  body: string;\n}\n\nexport function parsePassageFile(file: string, raw: string): ParsedFile {\n  let text = raw.replace(/^﻿/, \"\").replace(/\\r\\n/g, \"\\n\");\n  const meta: Record<string, string> = {};\n  const fm = /^---\\n([\\s\\S]*?)\\n---\\n?/.exec(text);\n  if (fm) {\n    for (const line of fm[1].split(\"\\n\")) {\n      const i = line.indexOf(\":\");\n      if (i > 0) meta[line.slice(0, i).trim().toLowerCase()] = line.slice(i + 1).trim().replace(/^[\"']|[\"']$/g, \"\");\n    }\n    text = text.slice(fm[0].length);\n  }\n  let title = meta.title;\n  const heading = /^# (.+)$/m.exec(text);\n  if (heading) {\n    if (!title) title = heading[1].trim();\n    text = text.replace(heading[0], \"\");\n  }\n  if (!title) title = path.basename(file, path.extname(file));\n  const body = text.trim();\n  const order = meta.sort_order ? Number(meta.sort_order) : NaN;\n  return {\n    file,\n    slug: slugify(title),\n    title,\n    author: meta.author || null,\n    cover_url: meta.cover || null,\n    sort_order: Number.isFinite(order) ? order : null,\n    body,\n    word_count: countWords(body),\n  };\n}\n\nasync function main(): Promise<void> {\n  const args = process.argv.slice(2).filter((a) => a !== \"--\");\n  const apply = args.includes(\"--apply\");\n  const folder = args.find((a) => !a.startsWith(\"--\"));\n  if (!folder || !fs.existsSync(folder) || !fs.statSync(folder).isDirectory()) {\n    console.error(\"usage: vite-node scripts/reading-center-import.ts -- <folder> [--apply]\");\n    process.exit(2);\n  }\n\n  const files = fs\n    .readdirSync(folder)\n    .filter((f) => /\\.(md|txt)$/i.test(f))\n    .sort((a, b) => a.localeCompare(b));\n  const parsed = files.map((f) => parsePassageFile(f, fs.readFileSync(path.join(folder, f), \"utf8\")));\n  const empty = parsed.filter((p) => !p.body);\n  if (empty.length) {\n    console.error(`Empty body in: ${empty.map((p) => p.file).join(\", \")}`);\n    process.exit(1);\n  }\n\n  const email = env.LSATPREP_ADMIN_EMAIL;\n  const password = [REDACTED];\n  if (!email || !password) {\n    console.error(\"reading-center-import: LSATPREP_ADMIN_EMAIL / LSATPREP_ADMIN_PASSWORD are not set in ~/ai-os/.env.\");\n    process.exit(2);\n  }\n  const { error: signInError } = await supabase.auth.signInWithPassword({ email, password });\n  if (signInError) {\n    console.error(`reading-center-import: sign-in failed: ${signInError.message}`);\n    process.exit(1);\n  }\n\n  const { data, error } = await rc(\"reading_center_passages\").select(\n    \"id, slug, title, author, cover_url, sort_order, body\",\n  );\n  let existing: ExistingRow[] = [];\n  if (error) {\n    if (apply) {\n      console.error(`Could not read passages: ${error.message}`);\n      process.exit(1);\n    }\n    console.warn(`! Could not read existing passages (${error.message}). Is the migration applied? Treating all as NEW.`);\n  } else {\n    existing = (data ?? []) as ExistingRow[];\n  }\n  const bySlug = new Map(existing.map((r) => [r.slug, r]));\n  let nextOrder = existing.reduce((m, r) => Math.max(m, r.sort_order), 0) + 1;\n\n  const totals = { NEW: 0, CHANGED: 0, SAME: 0 };\n  const writes: Record<string, unknown>[] = [];\n  for (const p of parsed) {\n    const row = bySlug.get(p.slug);\n    let status: keyof typeof totals;\n    if (!row) status = \"NEW\";\n    else\n      status =\n        row.body !== p.body ||\n        row.title !== p.title ||\n        (row.author ?? null) !== p.author ||\n        (row.cover_url ?? null) !== p.cover_url ||\n        (p.sort_order !== null && row.sort_order !== p.sort_order)\n          ? \"CHANGED\"\n          : \"SAME\";\n    totals[status]++;\n    console.log(`${status.padEnd(7)} ${p.title} (${p.word_count} words)  [${p.file}]`);\n    if (status === \"SAME\") continue;\n    writes.push({\n      slug: p.slug,\n      title: p.title,\n      author: p.author,\n      cover_url: p.cover_url,\n      body: p.body,\n      word_count: p.word_count,\n      sort_order: p.sort_order ?? row?.sort_order ?? nextOrder++,\n      updated_at: new Date().toISOString(),\n    });\n  }\n  console.log(`\\n${totals.NEW} new, ${totals.CHANGED} changed, ${totals.SAME} unchanged.`);\n\n  if (!apply) {\n    console.log(\"Dry run: nothing written. Re-run with --apply to write.\");\n    return;\n  }\n  if (writes.length === 0) return;\n  const { error: upsertError } = await rc(\"reading_center_passages\").upsert(writes, { onConflict: \"slug\" });\n  if (upsertError) {\n    console.error(`Upsert failed: ${upsertError.message}`);\n    process.exit(1);\n  }\n  console.log(`Wrote ${writes.length} passage(s).`);\n}\n\nmain().then(\n  () => process.exit(0),\n  (e) => {\n    console.error(e);\n    process.exit(1);\n  },\n);\n"}

## assistant

Steps 10 through 17 are built and committed on `feat/reading-center`, and the Tools room is in the Teacher Workspace. Typecheck, a full `npm run build` and the 14 tests all pass. I haven't seen any of it working in the browser yet: the dev server stops at the sign-in page, and I won't type your real admin password, even on localhost.

**What's built**
- **Tools room:** a new "Tools" tab at the end of the workspace tabs, after Insights, at `/teacher/tools`. Reading Center is its first card. Adding a future tool means adding one row to `src/pages/teacher/tools/TeacherToolsRoom.tsx`.
- **Pages:** library, stats and passage manager open inside the workspace. The reader opens full screen, like the Whiteboard. It sits at `.../read/<passage>`, so a passage titled "Stats" can't collide with the stats page. Everything is still admin-only.
- **Step 10 fixes:** all five reading modes now use your font, size and line spacing. Before, they ignored them, and some styles overrode line spacing. e-Reader page-turning was broken (invalid CSS) and its search skipped matches; both are fixed.
- **Steps 11–17:**
  - the reader controls: speed dialog, settings, full-text view, bookmarks, shortcuts, finish card, "pick up where you left off"
  - the reader page
  - the library
  - stats with goal setting
  - the passage manager
  - the bulk import script
- **Import script:** the dry run found my test file and printed one NEW line without writing anything. That was required, because the database tables don't exist yet.

**Where I departed from the plan**
- **Flow mode:** the plan's version looked exactly like Serial. Following the feature inventory, each chunk now appears at its real position on a line of about 10 words.
- **Reordering passages:** up and down arrows instead of drag-and-drop. Nothing in the app uses the drag library, so there was no pattern to copy.
- **Saving progress:** nothing is saved until you choose Resume or Start over, so opening a passage can't wipe saved progress.
- **Import credentials:** `assign.ts` looks for your `.env` in `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\.env`, which doesn't exist. Your keys are in `dev\ai-os\.env`. The import script now reads from there. `assign.ts` itself probably fails the same way, but I didn't touch it.

**Needs your attention**
- **Build side effect:** `npm run build` cut `supabase/functions/mcp/index.ts` down by 589 lines in the worktree. The Lovable MCP plugin rewrites that file during every build. My restore was denied, so it's sitting uncommitted. Run this to restore it:
```bash
git -C C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/lsatu-reading-center checkout -- supabase/functions/mcp/index.ts
```
- The shared `lsatu` checkout shows the same modified file, which probably has the same cause. I didn't touch it.
- **Migration:** still not applied. Until it is, the library will show "Could not load passages" and the reader can't open anything. Applying it changes the live Lovable Cloud database, so I need your yes right before I do it.

**Not done**
- Step 18 (final verification against the spec's acceptance checks) and Step 19 (push and open the PR). The branch is unpushed.
- A real browser test. I started the dev server at `http://localhost:8098` and added `lsatu-reading-center-dev` to `.claude/launch.json`. If you sign in there, I'll go through every mode, dialog and keyboard shortcut. That's only useful once the migration is applied.

## assistant

You're signed in now, so I got further. Everything that doesn't need the database works. Everything that does is blocked on the migration.

**What works**
- **Tools tab:** it's on the workspace rail, and the Tools room shows the Reading Center card. Clicking the card opens the library.
- **Library:** the header, Stats button, Manage passages button and search box all render. Then it shows "Could not load passages: Could not find the table 'public.reading_center_passages'". That's expected, because the migration hasn't been applied.
- **Stats:** the page renders with the period picker, the Set goal button and both speed gauges (current 0, target 600). That's the default goal, since your preferences can't be saved yet.
- **Manage passages:** the page renders. Its passage list will hit the same missing-table error.
- **Errors:** none in the browser console or the dev server log.

**One layout problem.** At the in-app browser's width, the rail now has 8 tabs. "Tools" is cut off at the right edge, and you have to scroll the rail to see it. On a wider screen it should fit. If you'd rather it never hides, I could shorten "Teaching Assistant" to "Assistant" (the header already has an Assistant button), or put Tools earlier in the order.

**The next step is the migration.** It creates the five `reading_center_*` tables plus the seed passage in the live Lovable Cloud database. Once it's in, I can test the reader itself: all five modes, the dialogs, bookmarks, resume and the keyboard shortcuts.

The Lovable Cloud rule says database changes go through Lovable. As far as I know, the free path is pasting the SQL into Lovable's SQL editor yourself, which costs no credits. Should I apply it, and by which route?

export function cn(...inputs: ClassValue[]) {

/**
 * Open an in-app route in a new tab, falling back to same-tab navigation when
 * the browser blocks the popup (e.g. inside embedded preview iframes like the
 * Lovable editor, where window.open returns null). Without the fallback, "Open"
 * buttons silently do nothing in those contexts.
 */
export function openRoute(route: string) {
  const win = window.open(route, '_blank', 'noopener,noreferrer');
  if (!win) window.location.assign(route);
}

import { useQuery, useMutation, useQueryClient, type UseQueryResult, type UseMutationResult } from "@tanstack/react-query";
import { useAuth } from "@/contexts/AuthContext";
import { rcDb } from "../lib/db";
import { DEFAULT_PREFERENCES } from "../lib/constants";
import { countWords } from "../lib/tokenize";
import type {
  Passage,
  Preferences,
  Progress,
  Bookmark,
  ReadingSession,
} from "../lib/types";

export function usePassages(): UseQueryResult<Passage[], Error> {
  const { user } = useAuth();
  const userId = user?.id;

  return useQuery({
    queryKey: ["reading-center", "passages"],
    queryFn: async (): Promise<Passage[]> => {
      const { data, error } = await rcDb("reading_center_passages")
        .select("*")
        .order("sort_order")
        .order("title");
      if (error) throw error;
      return (data ?? []) as Passage[];
    },
    enabled: !!userId,
  });
}

export function usePassageBySlug(slug: string | undefined): UseQueryResult<Passage | null, Error> {
  const { user } = useAuth();
  const userId = user?.id;

  return useQuery({
    queryKey: ["reading-center", "passage", slug],
    queryFn: async (): Promise<Passage | null> => {
      if (!slug) return null;
      const { data, error } = await rcDb("reading_center_passages")
        .select("*")
        .eq("slug", slug)
        .maybeSingle();
      if (error) throw error;
      return (data ?? null) as Passage | null;
    },
    enabled: !!userId && !!slug,
  });
}

export function useSavePreferences(): UseMutationResult<Preferences, Error, Partial<Preferences>> {
  const { user } = useAuth();
  const userId = user?.id;
  const queryClient = useQueryClient();

  return useMutation({
    mutationFn: async (input: Partial<Preferences>): Promise<Preferences> => {
      if (!userId) throw new Error("Not authenticated");
      const current =
        queryClient.getQueryData<Preferences>(["reading-center", "prefs", userId]) ?? DEFAULT_PREFERENCES;
      const merged: Preferences = { ...current, ...input };
      const payload = {
        user_id: userId,
        ...merged,
        updated_at: new Date().toISOString(),
      };
      const { error } = await rcDb("reading_center_preferences").upsert(payload, {
        onConflict: "user_id",
      });
      if (error) throw error;
      return merged;
    },
    onMutate: async (input: Partial<Preferences>) => {
      if (!userId) return;
      await queryClient.cancelQueries({ queryKey: ["reading-center", "prefs", userId] });
      const previous =
        queryClient.getQueryData<Preferences>(["reading-center", "prefs", userId]) ?? DEFAULT_PREFERENCES;
      const merged: Preferences = { ...previous, ...input };
      queryClient.setQueryData(["reading-center", "prefs", userId], merged);
      return { previous };
    },
    onError: (_err, _input, context) => {
      if (context?.previous && userId) {
        queryClient.setQueryData(["reading-center", "prefs", userId], context.previous);
      }
    },
    onSettled: () => {
      if (userId) {
        queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ["reading-center", "prefs", userId] });
      }
    },
  });
}

      if (!userId) return [];

export function useBookmarks(passageId: string | undefined): UseQueryResult<Bookmark[], Error> {
  const { user } = useAuth();
  const userId = user?.id;

  return useQuery({
    queryKey: ["reading-center", "bookmarks", userId, passageId],
    queryFn: async (): Promise<Bookmark[]> => {
      if (!userId || !passageId) return [];
      const { data, error } = await rcDb("reading_center_bookmarks")
        .select("*")
        .eq("user_id", userId)
        .eq("passage_id", passageId)
        .order("created_at", { ascending: false });
      if (error) throw error;
      return (data ?? []) as Bookmark[];
    },
    enabled: !!userId && !!passageId,
  });
}

export function useAddBookmark(): UseMutationResult<Bookmark, Error, { passage_id: string; word_index: number }> {
  const { user } = useAuth();
  const userId = user?.id;
  const queryClient = useQueryClient();

  return useMutation({
    mutationFn: async (input: { passage_id: string; word_index: number }): Promise<Bookmark> => {
      if (!userId) throw new Error("Not authenticated");
      const { data, error } = await rcDb("reading_center_bookmarks")
        .insert({
          user_id: userId,
          passage_id: input.passage_id,
          word_index: input.word_index,
        })
        .select()
        .single();
      if (error) throw error;
      return data as Bookmark;
    },
    onSuccess: (_data, vars) => {
      if (userId) {
        queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ["reading-center", "bookmarks", userId, vars.passage_id] });
      }
    },
  });
}

export function useDeleteBookmark(): UseMutationResult<void, Error, { id: string; passage_id?: string }> {
  const { user } = useAuth();
  const userId = user?.id;
  const queryClient = useQueryClient();

  return useMutation({
    mutationFn: async (input: { id: string; passage_id?: string }): Promise<void> => {
      if (!userId) throw new Error("Not authenticated");
      const { error } = await rcDb("reading_center_bookmarks")
        .delete()
        .eq("id", input.id);
      if (error) throw error;
    },
    onSuccess: (_data, vars) => {
      if (userId) {
        if (vars.passage_id) {
          queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ["reading-center", "bookmarks", userId, vars.passage_id] });
        } else {
          queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: ["reading-center", "bookmarks", userId] });
        }
      }
    },
  });
}

export function useSessions(sinceIso?: string): UseQueryResult<ReadingSession[], Error> {
  const { user } = useAuth();
  const userId = user?.id;

  return useQuery({
    queryKey: ["reading-center", "sessions", userId, sinceIso],
    queryFn: async (): Promise<ReadingSession[]> => {
      if (!userId) return [];
      let query = rcDb("reading_center_sessions")
        .select("*")
        .eq("user_id", userId);
      if (sinceIso) {
        query = query.gte("started_at", sinceIso);
      }
      query = query.order("started_at", { ascending: true });
      const { data, error } = await query;
      if (error) throw error;
      return (data ?? []) as ReadingSession[];
    },
    enabled: !!userId,
  });
}

export function useRecordSession(): UseMutationResult<void, Error, Omit<ReadingSession, "id">> {
  const { user } = useAuth();
  const userId = user?.id;
  const queryClient = useQueryClient();

  return useMutation({
    mutationFn: async (session: Omit<ReadingSession, "id">): Promise<void> => {
      if (!userId) throw new Error("Not authenticated");
      const { error } = await rcDb("reading_center_sessions").insert({
        user_id: userId,
        passage_id: session.passage_id,
        reading_mode: session.reading_mode,
        wpm: session.wpm,
        words_read: session.words_read,
        active_ms: session.active_ms,
        paused_ms: session.paused_ms,
        started_at: session.started_at,
        ended_at: session.ended_at,
      });

On integration into\n * LSAT U, hooks/useReadingCenterData.ts goes back to Supabase and this file\n * is deleted; nothing else reads it except the passage-manager delete check.\n */\ninterface Db {\n  version: 1;\n  passages: Passage[];\n  preferences: Preferences;\n  progress: Progress[];\n  bookmarks: Bookmark[];\n  sessions: ReadingSession[];\n}\n\nconst KEY = \"reading-center:v1\";\n\nfunction seedDb(): Db {\n  const now = new Date().toISOString();\n  return {\n    version: 1,\n    passages: [\n      {\n        id: crypto.randomUUID(),\n        slug: SEED_PASSAGE.slug,\n        title: SEED_PASSAGE.title,\n        author: null,\n        body: SEED_PASSAGE.body,\n        cover_url: null,\n        word_count: countWords(SEED_PASSAGE.body),\n        sort_order: 1,\n        is_published: true,\n        created_at: now,\n        updated_at: now,\n      },\n    ],\n    preferences: DEFAULT_PREFERENCES,\n    progress: [],\n    bookmarks: [],\n    sessions: [],\n  };\n}\n\nlet memory: Db | null = null;\n\nexport function readDb(): Db {\n  if (memory) return memory;\n  try {\n    const raw = localStorage.getItem(KEY);\n    if (raw) {\n      const parsed = JSON.parse(raw) as Db;\n      memory = { ...seedDb(), ...parsed, preferences: { ...DEFAULT_PREFERENCES, ...parsed.preferences } };\n      return memory;\n    }\n  } catch {\n    // Blocked or corrupt storage: fall through to a fresh in-memory copy.\n  }\n  memory = seedDb();\n  writeDb(memory);\n  return memory;\n}\n\nexport function writeDb(next: Db): void {\n  memory = next;\n  try {\n    localStorage.setItem(KEY, JSON.stringify(next));\n  } catch {\n    // Storage full or blocked: keep working from memory for this tab.\n  }\n}\n\nexport function updateDb(fn: (db: Db) => Db): Db {\n  const next = fn(readDb());\n  writeDb(next);\n  return next;\n}\n\nexport function progressCountForPassage(passageId: string): number {\n  return readDb().progress.filter((p) => p.passage_id === passageId && p.word_index > 0).length;\n}\n"}

queryKey: [\"reading-center\", \"bookmarks\"] }),\n  });\n}\n\nexport function useSessions(sinceIso?: string): UseQueryResult<ReadingSession[], Error> {\n  return useQuery({\n    queryKey: [\"reading-center\", \"sessions\", sinceIso],\n    queryFn: async (): Promise<ReadingSession[]> =>\n      readDb()\n        .sessions.filter((s) => !sinceIso || s.started_at >= sinceIso)\n        .sort((a, b) => a.started_at.localeCompare(b.started_at)),\n  });\n}\n\nexport function useRecordSession(): UseMutationResult<void, Error, Omit<ReadingSession, \"id\">> {\n  const queryClient = useQueryClient();\n  return useMutation({\n    mutationFn: async (session: Omit<ReadingSession, \"id\">): Promise<void> => {\n      updateDb((db) => ({ ...db, sessions: [...db.sessions, { id: crypto.randomUUID(), ...session }] }));\n    },\n    onSuccess: () => queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: [\"reading-center\", \"sessions\"] }),\n  });\n}\n\nexport function useUpsertPassage(): UseMutationResult<\n  Passage,\n  Error,\n  Partial<Passage> & { title: string; body: string }\n> {\n  const queryClient = useQueryClient();\n  return useMutation({\n    mutationFn: async (input: Partial<Passage> & { title: string; body: string }): Promise<Passage> => {\n      const now = new Date().toISOString();\n      let saved: Passage | null = null;\n      updateDb((db) => {\n        const existing = input.id ? db.passages.find((p) => p.id === input.id) : undefined;\n        const slug = existing?.slug ?? input.slug ?? slugify(input.title);\n        if (!existing && db.passages.some((p) => p.slug === slug)) {\n          throw new Error(`A passage with the address \"${slug}\" already exists. Change the title.`);\n        }\n        saved = {\n          id: existing?.id ?? crypto.randomUUID(),\n          author: null,\n          cover_url: null,\n          is_published: true,\n          sort_order: db.passages.reduce((m, p) => Math.max(m, p.sort_order), 0) + 1,\n          created_at: now,\n          ...existing,\n          ...input,\n          slug,\n          word_count: countWords(input.body),\n          updated_at: now,\n        } as Passage;\n        return { ...db, passages: [...db.passages.filter((p) => p.id !== saved!.id), saved] };\n      });\n      return saved!;\n    },\n    onSuccess: () => queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: [\"reading-center\"] }),\n  });\n}\n\nexport function useDeletePassage(): UseMutationResult<void, Error, string> {\n  const queryClient = useQueryClient();\n  return useMutation({\n    mutationFn: async (id: string): Promise<void> => {\n      // Mirrors the on-delete-cascade in the LSAT U migration.\n      updateDb((db) => ({\n        ...db,\n        passages: db.passages.filter((p) => p.id !== id),\n        progress: db.progress.filter((p) => p.passage_id !== id),\n        bookmarks: db.bookmarks.filter((b) => b.passage_id !== id),\n        sessions: db.sessions.filter((s) => s.passage_id !== id),\n      }));\n    },\n    onSuccess: () => queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: [\"reading-center\"] }),\n  });\n}\n\nexport function useReorderPassages(): UseMutationResult<void, Error, { id: string; sort_order: number }[]> {\n  const queryClient = useQueryClient();\n  return useMutation({\n    mutationFn: async (items: { id: string; sort_order: number }[]): Promise<void> => {\n      const order = new Map(items.map((i) => [i.id, i.sort_order]));\n      updateDb((db) => ({\n        ...db,\n        passages: db.passages.map((p) => (order.has(p.id) ? { ...p, sort_order: order.get(p.id)! } : p)),\n      }));\n    },\n    onSuccess: () => queryClient.invalidateQueries({ queryKey: [\"reading-center\", \"passages\"] }),\n  });\n}\n"}

## assistant

Reading Center now runs as its own web app at **http://localhost:8100**, completely separate from LSAT U. It works right away: passages, settings and progress save in your browser, so it doesn't need the database migration.

**Checked in the browser**
- **Library:** shows the seed passage. "Living in a rational society", 550 words.
- **Serial:** plays, and Space pauses it. Progress saved at the word I stopped on.
- **Settings:** the S key opens them. Switching to Highlight kept my place in the passage.
- **Resume:** reopening the passage asked "Pick up where you left off?" and Resume returned to the saved word.
- **e-Reader:** pages turn with the arrow keys (1/3, then 2/3), and the text continues cleanly across the break.
- **Flow:** each chunk appears at its real place on the line.
- **Scroll:** the current line sits on the guide line.
- **Build:** typecheck, 14/14 tests and a production build all pass.

**Not checked by hand:** bookmarks, the View panel, the WPM dialog, the Stats charts with real data, and the passage manager (adding, editing, reordering, deleting). They're built but untested. Stats will stay empty until you've done some reading.

**What else changed**
- **LSAT U:** I took the Tools room back out of the `feat/reading-center` branch. The feature code is still on that branch, unused, ready for when we add it to LSAT U.
- **New project:** the app is at `dev/ai-os-projects/reading-center`, in its own local git repo with no GitHub remote yet. Its reading feature is the same code as the LSAT U branch. Only the save layer and the page addresses differ, so integrating later means swapping those two files back.
- **Small fix:** the slider tracks were nearly invisible, so I darkened them.
- **Restart config:** `reading-center-dev` is in `.claude/launch.json` if the server ever needs restarting.

**When it goes into LSAT U:** "assign" will mean turning on access to the whole tool for a student, as you said. That's also when the database migration gets applied.

**Still open**
1. **Build side effect on the LSAT U branch:** the earlier LSAT U build mangled `supabase/functions/mcp/index.ts` in the `lsatu-reading-center` worktree, and that change is still uncommitted. The restore command from before still works:
   ```bash
   git -C C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/lsatu-reading-center checkout -- supabase/functions/mcp/index.ts
   ```
2. **Delete-passage wording:** it still says "N students have progress". In a one-person app it should say "You have progress".
3. **Duplicate links:** the Library page repeats the Stats and Manage links that are already in the top nav. I left both in because the page is shared with the LSAT U copy.

# REA

Use REA when a claim depends on a shipped binary or package, decompilation,
passive application runtime evidence, or comparison with
behavior not established by available source. For ordinary analysis of a
complete source repository, use normal repository tools and do not run REA
readiness or provider commands.

## Connect only when needed

Installing this skill supplies instructions; it does not register the REA MCP
server, install analysis engines, or add tools to an already-running agent.

If REA tools are available and their registration is not known to be stale,
proceed directly to the target. Do not run diagnostics or setup before every
investigation. Use the connected server's actual tool list and input schemas;
a skill installed from repository main may describe capabilities absent from
an older npm release. Keep complete inline Evidence when that server does not
advertise retained references.

If tools are absent, a call is blocked by the client, or a required provider is
unavailable, read [connection and recovery](references/connection-and-recovery.md).
Diagnose the observed blocker, continue useful supported work, and resume the
original task after repair. Client approval, shell PATH, registration and engine
availability have different remedies; avoid repeating setup for all of them.

Prefer an existing `rea` CLI. When none is installed and REA package acquisition
is within the authorized scope, use `npx -y rea-agents@6.4.0` in its place.
Configuration repair starts with a read-only setup plan; show paths, backups and
skill replacement before applying authorized changes. Preserve existing approval
and target-execution boundaries. REA setup never installs or upgrades unrelated
software, including Node.js, npm, Homebrew, Java, Ghidra, IDA, JADX, Binwalk or Unblob.

## Route the target first

Choose the first tool from the target the user supplied. Use `open_binary` for
active-target native or archive workflows; target-free tools take their own
explicit path or endpoint and do not need it.

- ASAR or extracted JavaScript/Electron tree:
  `analyze_javascript_application`.
- Archive/package member inventory (ZIP/APK/IPA/MSIX/AppX or DMG):
  `open_binary` with the supplied local path; use `inspect_artifact` when its
  graph and findings help answer the question.
- Android APK code, classes, methods, or incoming references:
  `inspect_android_package`, then focused Android tools when advertised.
  Archive member inventory still uses the archive route above.
- Managed PE/CLI assembly: `inspect_managed_artifact`.
- Firmware image: `inspect_firmware_regions` when advertised. Use
  `extract_firmware` when extraction is requested, with a caller-selected new
  absolute output directory. These tools use caller-supplied Binwalk/Unblob on
  Linux; see the [firmware guide](https://github.com/morluto/rea/blob/main/docs/firmware-analysis.md).
- .NET NativeAOT PE/ELF: native Ghidra analysis. Native code can yield pseudocode;
  consult the NativeAOT workflow in `references/native-and-artifacts.md` for
  optional metadata recovery and its supported host/layout boundary.
- User-owned browser page already open: `list_browser_targets`.
- Retained HAR/native mitmproxy capture: `inspect_web_network_capture`. Select
  original record ordinals when useful; inspect producer fields and byte/number
  sidecars without fetching recorded URLs or claiming live attribution. See
  [historical captures](https://github.com/morluto/rea/blob/main/docs/web-network-captures.md) for the exact upstream
  profile and credential exclusions.
- User-owned Electron runtime already open: `list_electron_targets`.
- Explicit local EVM bytecode carrier: `inspect_evm_interface` with caller-selected
  `raw` or `hex` encoding. Preserve carrier/decoded digests and treat selectors,
  argument strings and mutability as inferred candidates; this performs no chain
  lookup or target execution. See the
  [EVM bytecode guide](https://github.com/morluto/rea/blob/main/docs/evm-bytecode.md).
- Explicit Linux ELF file for offline layout, symbols, relocations or static
  mitigation evidence: `inspect_binary_layout`. This target-free operation uses
  caller-supplied pwntools without opening a disassembler database. Preserve its
  raw locations and inference/coverage limits; see the
  [offline binary guide](https://github.com/morluto/rea/blob/main/docs/binary-diagnostics.md).
- Supplied Linux x86-64 ELF core: `inspect_recorded_crash`. Read every recorded
  thread, raw note and source-bound register without opening a live target.
  Historical PIDs do not select live processes. Request `include_debugger_context`
  only when core-only mapping candidates help; see the
  [recorded crash guide](https://github.com/morluto/rea/blob/main/docs/recorded-crashes.md).
- PE resource type/name/language trees and static icon group references:
  `inspect_pe_resources` with an explicit path. This portable operation requires
  no active target or engine; opaque payloads retain file ranges and SHA-256.
- Native executable, library, or analysis database: `open_binary`, then
  use focused analysis tools directly; call `binary_overview` when metadata or
  inventory context is useful and available from the selected provider.

For an existing IDA MCP configuration, use `open_binary` with the original
input binary and `provider_id: "ida"`; do not pass an `.idb` or `.i64` database.
The legacy attached profile binds the already-open GUI input by SHA-256 and
leaves its database open. The modern headless profile creates and closes an
owned private database without saving. Installation and registration reuse
[mrexodia/ida-pro-mcp](https://github.com/mrexodia/ida-pro-mcp); see the
[IDA provider guide](https://github.com/morluto/rea/blob/main/docs/ida-provider.md).
Proceed directly to `analyze_function`, pseudocode, or function/string searches
after opening; IDA does not supply `binary_overview`. Consult session availability
when composing broader workflows. Modern direct callers and unsupported dossier
facets remain unknown; live IDA results are not replayed from snapshots.

If the user says only "my app" without a name, path, or target already selected
for this task, ask which app before searching for artifacts. Paths in unrelated
saved reports and nearby test fixtures do not select the user's target. Resolve
an actual human-readable app name to one clear installed artifact when possible;
ask when matches are ambiguous. Never choose an example app on the user's behalf.

In a target-free session, use `open_binary` to bind any archive/package or
native target whose analysis tool operates on the active target. Do not call an
unadvertised tool; inspect `binary_session` with `{}` and its
`result.tool_availability` for availability reasons and remediation. The tool
list stays complete; availability depends on the operation, target, and host.

## Work summary-first

Start with the default result and use its inline Evidence and graph context.
For a JavaScript application or ELF layout whose size is unknown or large,
call `analyze_javascript_application` or `inspect_binary_layout` with
`"detail": "summary"`, then inspect the retained `parent_evidence_id` with
`inspect_analysis_view`.
Page module identities and select the returned `node_id` before requesting an
item; a single file path can represent several graph roles. Do not repeat an
identical analysis or rejected call without a changed input or resolved blocker.
Recheck session state when a lifecycle change makes that status useful.
Make a focused follow-up only when the
returned result leaves a specific question unanswered.

If MCP reports `resource_constraint` with `details.resource: "transport"`, call
`inspect_analysis_view` with its reported same-session Evidence reference for a
summary, one section/module, or a stable page. Use `trace_application_feature`
once a seed is known, or call `export_evidence_bundle` to write the complete
session to a selected path. The analysis remains complete in the ledger; avoid
repeating it merely to request the same oversized response. Complete CLI JSON
output also streams without a single MCP frame.

When the retained parent ID is unknown, use `get_evidence_bundle` with
`detail: "summary"` and exact filters for operation, target SHA-256,
analysis-profile digest or canonical procedure address. Select its returned
Evidence ID for the next view instead of requesting the complete bundle.
`retention: "complete-record"` means the whole record is stored; native
value-flow truncation and unknown metadata remain separate. For an exported
portable bundle, use `rea inspect-evidence-bundle` with JSON containing `path`,
`detail: "summary"` and the same `filters`. Reading that file still requires
validating its full contents within the local memory budget.

A client can also truncate a successful tool result without a REA transport
error. Treat that preview as incomplete; use the retained parent ID for smaller
views or export when the full record is needed. Do not parse the preview as
canonical JSON or repeat the producer. For JavaScript, read the
[module-view and trace recipes](references/javascript-applications.md) before
drilling into a large graph; a broad bidirectional trace can expand unrelated
containment relationships even after summary analysis.

Every conclusion must distinguish observations, inferences, and unknowns. Cite
Evidence IDs, preserve limitations and incomplete coverage, and never imply
that static analysis observed execution. Runtime requests execute the declared
target and lifecycle; do not broaden the target or action beyond those fields.

Within the user's requested investigation, call available analysis tools
directly. REA does not require permission grants or per-call approval flags.
The agent client can independently require approval. Follow the declared request
scope and the host's actual access requirements; use the recovery guide for an
observed client-policy rejection.

## Plan broader investigations

For requests that span multiple features or subsystems, use a staged workflow:

1. Turn the request into a checklist of questions and the evidence each answer
   needs. Resolve target identity and constraints from the conversation and
   workspace before asking for information again.
2. Inspect the current REA session, artifact identity, saved analysis database,
   bookmarks, and prior evidence. Reuse matching state; do not open duplicate
   sessions or repeat identical analysis.
3. Start with the smallest useful overview or inventory. Follow each question
   from its entry point through relevant data and state changes to its result.
   Batch related operations around a specific hypothesis, then expand only when
   the returned evidence leaves a concrete gap.
4. Inspect relevant packaged resources and configuration alongside code when
   they affect the question. Use format-aware inventory and parsers; do not
   infer behavior from filenames, strings, or layout alone.
5. Corroborate a conclusion with the evidence type it requires. Use runtime
   observation only when static evidence cannot answer the question and the
   required host runtime and OS access are available. For JavaScript behavior,
   run probes against the actual app through the available browser, Electron,
   or process capture workflow.
6. Decompose work into independent questions. When parallel workers are
   available and the questions do not depend on one another, assign distinct
   scopes, point workers to existing evidence, and ask them to return sources,
   conclusions, and unresolved gaps. Otherwise, work sequentially.
7. Keep a concise finding ledger linking each conclusion to Evidence IDs,
   confidence/evidence type, search boundary, and remaining unknowns. Update
   the shared index or investigation report so later passes can reuse results.

Before finishing, revisit the original checklist. Mark each question as
answered, partially answered, or unresolved based on its evidence; keep
bounded negative searches bounded, and do not describe a broad investigation
as complete while required questions remain open.

## Read only the relevant guide

- Native binaries, managed assemblies, archives, and extraction:
  [references/native-and-artifacts.md](references/native-and-artifacts.md)
- ASARs, extracted JavaScript, feature tracing, and version comparison:
  [references/javascript-applications.md](references/javascript-applications.md)
- Android APK declarations, classes, methods, and static references:
  [references/android-applications.md](references/android-applications.md)
- Passive browser/Electron observation and static/runtime reconciliation:
  [references/runtime-observation.md](references/runtime-observation.md)
- Evidence paging, comparisons, residual unknowns, and verification:
  [references/evidence-workflows.md](references/evidence-workflows.md)

## Finish the task

Explain findings in plain language and tie them to returned evidence. When the
user asks to build something, use normal coding tools and separate observed
behavior from design choices. Close an opened native session with
`close_binary` when the investigation is complete.

# Spreeder feature inventory

Verified live unless marked *(code only)*. "Code only" means the name exists in the app's main bundle
(`resources.ereflect.com/Spreeder/webApp/main.21893fdef5a7593c.js`) but no screen for it was reachable
from this account.

## 1. Library (`/library`)

- Shelves: **Recent**, **Added By Me** (with "View All").
- Card: cover image (generic document icon for user text), title, author line.
- Card ⋮ menu: **View/Location**, **Edit Image**, **Edit Text**, **Tag**, **Delete**, **Hide from Recent**.
- Global search: "Searching 20504 books.." (built-in catalogue) plus a book-search icon.
- Routes: `library`, `library/:name`, `library/tags/:name`.

## 2. Adding text (Add Text button)

| Option | Behaviour |
|---|---|
| Paste Text | Full-screen "Add To Library": Enter Title field, Add Text Here box, Cancel / Save (Save disabled until filled). |
| Import Document | Opens a file picker (accepted types not exposed). |
| AI Book Summary | Uses AI credits; generates a cover step, falls back to text only if the cover fails. |
| AI Generate | Uses AI credits. |

Profile shows the credit balance ("0 AI Credits").

## 3. Reader (`/spreed`)

### Chrome around the text
- Top: Back, title, **Search** (e-Reader only), **Fullscreen**, **Bookmarks**, **Settings (gear)**.
- Bottom: **Keyboard shortcuts** button, **WPM button** (shows current speed), **View**, progress ("1% - 3 minutes remaining").
- Transport: previous chunk ◁, play/pause, next chunk ▷.

### Speed (WPM) dialog
- "Reading Speed (WPM)" with **−100, −10, +10, +100** buttons, Cancel / Save. Default 200.

### View panel
- Full passage overlay with "User added" badge, **Search this text**, current word boxed, click any word to jump,
  play button, footer "3 minutes to go - 200 WPM".

### Bookmarks
- Empty state: "To create a bookmark, simply pause Spreeder while you're reading and long press or click on any word."

### Keyboard shortcuts
| Keys | Action |
|---|---|
| ↑ / ↓ or + / − | increase / decrease speed |
| P or Space | pause / resume |
| → | section forward |
| ← | section back |
| R | restart |
| S | settings |

## 4. Preferences dialog ("<name>'s preferences")

Tabs: **Spreed · Appearance · Advanced**, plus **Restore Defaults**. Saved per user, server-side.

### Spreed tab: Reading Mode and its per-mode settings
| Mode | What it looks like | Settings (ranges) |
|---|---|---|
| **Serial** | One chunk flashed in a fixed spot | Number of Lines (1–6), Words Per Line (1–6), Center Text (toggle, default on) |
| **Flow** | Chunk flashes, positioned where it sits in the line | Words Per Line (1–6) |
| **Highlight** | Whole text visible, current chunk bright, rest dimmed | Words Per Line (1–6), Number of Lines (1–6) |
| **Scroll** | Text scrolls past | Words Per Line (1–6) |
| **e-Reader** | Normal paginated reading ("1 / 6"), page slider, prev/next page, in-text search | Theme, Font, Size, Line Spacing (1.0–2.0, step 0.1) |

### Appearance tab (all modes)
- **Theme:** Light, Dark, High Contrast, System.
- **Font (19):** Arial, Times New Roman, Verdana, Calibri, Microsoft Sans Serif, Impact, Lucida Console,
  Comic Sans MS, Georgia, Palatino Linotype, Tahoma, Trebuchet MS, Courier New, Open Dyslexic Font, Antikor,
  Code New Roman, CQ Mono, Pointfree, Fira Mono.
- **Size:** 8, 9, 10, 11, 12, 14, 16, 18, 20, 22, 24, 28, 32, 36, 40, 44, 50, 56, 62, 68, 74 (default 36).
- Live "Sample Text" preview.
- Note: in speed modes this tab renders blank (Spreeder bug); the controls exist in the DOM and show
  correctly in e-Reader mode and under Profile → Settings.

### Advanced tab
- **Punctuation Pauses** (default on): "Slight pause at end of sentences and punctuation marks. Only works for group size of 1."
- **Speed Variability** (default off): no help text shown. *Inference:* varies display time per chunk (e.g. by word length).

## 5. Profile (`/profile`)

- Header: name, AI credit balance, share button.
- **Dashboard:** Vital Stats gauges (Current Speed, Target Speed, 0–2000 scale), Reading Focus, Training Time,
  Reading Speed charts, each with refresh and a period picker.
- **Stats:** metric picker (Reading Speed, Reading Focus, Training Time) × period (24 Hours, 7 Days, 14 Days, 30 Days, 6 Months).
- **Options:** Theme (submenu), Edit Profile, **Goals** (Reading Speed Goal slider 50–5000 WPM, default 600,
  with Recommend button; copy: "An average person reads at 200 words per minute. We recommend starting with a
  goal of 600 WPM."), Settings (same Appearance + Advanced dialog), Sign Out.

## 6. Courses (`/courses`)

- Empty for this account: "No curriculums or courses assigned to your class yet."
- *(code only)* curricula → courses → steps with replay and preview; Nearpod activities; class invites; admin.

## 7. Present in code, not reachable here *(code only)*

- Training games: Speed Builder, Focus, Catch, Falling Words, Rising Words, Sinking Words, Letter Train I/II,
  Key Memory, Key React, Quick Trainer; Typesy-imported games.
- Word work: word lists with learn, review, edit, word-scroll; flashcards; quizzes.
- Text-to-speech: voices and voice rate (browser speechSynthesis), voice control.
- Streaks.

## 8. Not inspected

- Fullscreen (standard browser fullscreen assumed), Edit Profile form, Import Document accepted file types,
  AI Book Summary / AI Generate dialogs (both need credits; account has 0), Tags page contents, eReflect app grid.

## 9. First predetermined passage

**Living in a rational society** (user added, ~550 words, ~3 min at 200 WPM)

The rationalizing of society can be conceptualized as the pursuit of efficiency, predictability, calculability, and control through technology. But rational systems inevitably spawn a series of irrationalities that result in the compromising and perhaps even the undermining of their rationality. Fast-food restaurants, which epitomize the rational model, proffer the fastest means of getting from a hungry state to a sated one, without surprises, at low cost, in a carnival-like setting suggesting that fun awaits the consumer at each visit. The wholesomeness of the food seems an insignificant consideration. Whereas in the past, working people were prepared to spend up to an hour preparing dinner, they now are impatient if a meal is not on the table within ten minutes. (For their part, some fast-food restaurants have developed chairs that become uncomfortable after about twenty minutes, to ensure that diners do not stay long.) Fast-food restaurants have preferentially recruited adolescent help, at least until recently, because this age group adjusts more easily than adults do to surrendering their autonomy to machines, rules, and procedures. Few skills are required on the job, so workers are asked to use only a minute portion of their abilities. This policy is irrational from the standpoint of the organization, since it could obtain much more from its employees for the money (however negligible) it pays them. These minimal skill demands are also irrational from the perspective of the employees, who are not allowed to think or to respond creatively to the demands of the work. These restrictions lead to high levels of resentment, job dissatisfaction, alienation, absenteeism, and turnover among workers in fast-food franchises. In fact, these businesses have the highest turnover rate of any industry in the U.S. The entire workforce of the fast-food industry turns over three times in a year. Although the simple, repetitive nature of the work makes it easy to replace those who leave, the organization would clearly benefit from keeping employees longer. The costs of hiring and training are magnified when the turnover rate is extraordinarily high.

The application of the rational model to the house-building process in the 1950s and ’60s led to suburban communities consisting of nearly identical structures. Indeed, it was possible to wander into the residence of someone else and not to realize immediately that one was not at home. The more expensive developments were superficially more diversified, but their interior layouts assumed residents who were indistinguishable in their requirements. Furthermore, the planned communities themselves look very similar. Established trees are bulldozed to facilitate construction. In their place, a number of saplings, held up by posts and wire, are planted. Streets are laid out in symmetrical grid patterns. With such uniformity, suburbanites may well enter the wrong subdivision or become lost in their own.

Many of Steven Spielberg’s films are set in such suburbs. Spielberg’s strategy is to lure the viewer into this highly repetitive world and then to have a completely unexpected event occur. For example, the film Poltergeist takes place in a conventional suburban household in which evil spirits ultimately disrupt the sameness. (The spirits first manifest themselves through another key element of the homogeneous society—the television set.) The great success of Spielberg’s films may be traceable to a longing for some unpredictability, even if it is bizarre and menacing, in increasingly routinized lives.

Paragraph breaks are inferred; Spreeder's word view does not preserve them.

{"file_path": "C:/Users/Joshu/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/95fbd50f-6898-4412-a19a-7fd02386eb44/scratchpad/collector.py", "content": "\"\"\"Local-only receiver: the Spreeder tab POSTs captures here; each is written to OUT/<name>.\"\"\"\nimport http.server, os, re, sys\n\nOUT = sys.argv[1]\nos.makedirs(OUT, exist_ok=True)\n\n\nclass H(http.server.BaseHTTPRequestHandler):\n    def _cors(self):\n        self.send_header(\"Access-Control-Allow-Origin\", \"https://spreeder.ereflect.com\")\n        self.send_header(\"Access-Control-Allow-Methods\", \"POST, OPTIONS\")\n        self.send_header(\"Access-Control-Allow-Headers\", \"content-type\")\n        self.send_header(\"Access-Control-Allow-Private-Network\", \"true\")\n\n    def do_OPTIONS(self):\n        self.send_response(204)\n        self._cors()\n        self.end_headers()\n\n    def do_POST(self):\n        name = re.sub(r\"[^A-Za-z0-9_.-]\", \"_\", self.path.strip(\"/\")) or \"capture.txt\"\n        body = self.rfile.read(int(self.headers.get(\"content-length\", 0)))\n        with open(os.path.join(OUT, name), \"wb\") as f:\n            f.write(body)\n        self.send_response(200)\n        self._cors()\n        self.end_headers()\n        self.wfile.write(b\"ok\")\n\n    def log_message(self, *a):\n        pass\n\n\nhttp.server.HTTPServer((\"127.0.0.1\", 8765), H).serve_forever()\n"}

{"file_path": "C:/Users/Joshu/AppData/Local/Temp/claude/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/95fbd50f-6898-4412-a19a-7fd02386eb44/scratchpad/extract_components.py", "content": "\"\"\"Split Spreeder's main.js into per-component records: selector, CSS, and the\nstatic template strings (text and attribute constants) Angular compiled in.\"\"\"\nimport json, os, re, sys\n\nsrc, out_dir = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2]\njs = open(src, encoding=\"utf-8\").read()\nos.makedirs(out_dir, exist_ok=True)\n\nsel_re = re.compile(r'selectors:\\[\\[\"([a-z0-9-]+)\"\\]\\]')\nhits = [(m.start(), m.group(1)) for m in sel_re.finditer(js)]\n\n\ndef read_js_string(s, i):\n    \"\"\"Read a JS string literal starting at s[i] (quote char). Returns (value, end).\"\"\"\n    q = s[i]\n    j = i + 1\n    buf = []\n    while j < len(s):\n        c = s[j]\n        if c == \"\\\\\":\n            buf.append(s[j:j + 2]); j += 2; continue\n        if c == q:\n            break\n        buf.append(c); j += 1\n    raw = \"\".join(buf)\n    try:\n        val = json.loads('\"' + raw.replace('\"', '\\\\\"').replace(\"\\\\'\", \"'\") + '\"')\n    except Exception:\n        val = raw\n    return val, j + 1\n\n\nindex = []\nfor n, (pos, name) in enumerate(hits):\n    end = hits[n + 1][0] if n + 1 < len(hits) else len(js)\n    # A component definition runs from its selectors to its styles/closing; bound by the next selector.\n    seg = js[pos:end]\n    styles = []\n    sm = re.search(r'styles:\\[', seg)\n    if sm:\n        k = sm.end()\n        while k < len(seg) and seg[k] in \"\\\"'`\":\n            val, k = read_js_string(seg, k)\n            styles.append(val)\n            if k < len(seg) and seg[k] == \",\":\n                k += 1\n    # Template text nodes: ɵɵtext(n, \"...\") compiles to e.EFF(n,\"text\") / t.EFF(...) -> catch any (num,\"string\") pairs\n    texts = sorted(set(m.group(1) for m in re.finditer(r'\\(\\d+,\\s*\"([^\"\\\\]{2,120})\"\\)', seg)))\n    # Static class/attribute consts\n    consts = sorted(set(m.group(1) for m in re.finditer(r'\"class\",\"([^\"]{1,160})\"', seg)))\n    ids = sorted(set(m.group(1) for m in re.finditer(r'\"id\",\"([^\"]{1,80})\"', seg)))\n    css = \"\\n\".join(styles).replace(\"[_ngcontent-%COMP%]\", \"\").replace(\"[_nghost-%COMP%]\", \":host\")\n    rec = {\"selector\": name, \"offset\": pos, \"css_chars\": len(css), \"texts\": texts, \"classes\": consts, \"ids\": ids}\n    with open(os.path.join(out_dir, f\"{name}.css\"), \"w\", encoding=\"utf-8\") as f:\n        f.write(css)\n    with open(os.path.join(out_dir, f\"{name}.json\"), \"w\", encoding=\"utf-8\") as f:\n        json.dump(rec, f, indent=1, ensure_ascii=False)\n    index.append((name, len(css), len(texts)))\n\nwith open(os.path.join(out_dir, \"_index.txt\"), \"w\", encoding=\"utf-8\") as f:\n    for name, c, t in index:\n        f.write(f\"{name}\\t{c}\\t{t}\\n\")\nprint(len(index), \"components\")\n"}
